# Advent primer: source audit

Second version, collated on 2026-11-19 (UTC) by the binding lane from the writer's claims ledger (`claims.md`, `claims-quotes.md`, the two fact ledgers and the fix-pass ledger), the accuracy review, and the library records under `src/sources/`. One row per claim the book makes: every quotation (matched by script, word for word, to the record named) and every statement of fact around the quotations. Rows are ordered by chapter file and line; a key begins `c` and the file's number. The bound records, their states and fingerprints are in `source-bindings.toml` beside this file.

Evidence states:

- **verified source text**: the record named is verified, and the wording or fact stands in its checked transcription (or, for page-image passages, in the record's verbatim context).
- **checked quotation or paraphrase**: the wording was checked against an exact witness short of a verified record: a tracked Scripture verse text, an inspected web text, or a page image read for a record not verified.
- **source-grounded synthesis**: a statement inferred, combined or computed from the named records.
- **editorial or AI proposal**: the book's own rendering, arrangement, counsel or scope statement. Where it renders a Latin text, the record named holds that Latin.
- **unverified lead**: a statement resting on a reading not registered as a library record.

Standing qualifications that apply to every row that cites them:

- **AAS 52 (1960 rubrics).** The records say "read on the microfilm page image". The vatican.va "-ocr.pdf" pages are ABBYY re-typesetting, not facsimiles; a microfilm facsimile is registered for pp. 589-745. Which rendering each earlier AAS 52 record was read on is not settled here. The new record for n. 299 (Rubricae generales Missalis) was transcribed on 2026-11-19 from that registered microfilm facsimile, printed p. 648 (PDF p. 60).
- **Latin of Gregory's homilies.** The Latin of Homilies 1 and 8 is now verified in Migne's Patrologia Latina 76 (Garnier reprint, 1878): Hom. 1.1, 1.5 and 8.1. The day-and-place heading of Homily 7 rests on the Wikisource Latin, inspected only; that of Homily 20 is verified in PL 76 col. 1159, where Migne notes that four manuscripts lack its day. The English of Gregory is Bute's or the Catena's.
- **Ambrose.** The Latin of the Expositio in Lucam is quoted from Schenkl's text (CSEL 32/4, 1902), with *v* printed for Schenkl's consonantal *u*. His English is Bute's or the Catena's, or the book's own (listed below).
- **Catena Aurea.** Its excerpts join or condense passages; attribute such text "as the Catena gives it". The Catena's "Cyril" on Luke is Cyril of Alexandria, whose Commentary on Luke, Sermon 1, in R. Payne Smith's translation (Oxford, 1859), carries the passage on Luke 2:7; no Payne Smith record is held. Its "Bede" is Bede's In Lucam I (PL 92).
- **Bute 1908.** It prints the pre-1960 nine-lesson Office. Its homily numbering is unreliable (it heads Hom. 6 as "10th"); the book cites Gregory's homilies and Leo's sermons by the Patrologia Latina numbering.
- **The 1962 Office.** No printed 1961-62 Breviary was read. The one-nocturn form is stated from the rubrics (nn. 162 a, 220, 221 a, 222 a). The Isaiah course is that of the 1888 Breviary.
- **The 1962 Mass.** The Latin of the Collects is the Latin of the Roman Missal of 1962 as Triptych's edited text gives it: the 1862 Pustet wording read against the 1962 typical edition (records `triptych.roman-1962-latin-proper-editorial-projection`). The English of the Collects is Lasance's (1945). Stations and readings of the 1962 Missal rest on the facsimile, read on page images (station headings of Advent I, the Vigil and Christmas also in a verified text-free control record).
- **The book's own translations.** Short renderings of Latin in the running text, and these passages, are the book's own English (Appendix C): Ambrose on the manger and the altars and on the swaddling bands (Expos. in Luc. II.41-42); Bernard, In Adventu V.1; Gregory of Tours, Hist. Franc. X.31; Amalarius; John Beleth; Honorius Augustodunensis; William Durandus; Gregory, Hom. 8 (grass turned to wheat); Augustine, Sermons 185 and 191; Leo, *Semper enim virtuti cibus jejunium fuit* (Sermo 13). Their rows are classed editorial; the Latin is verified in the record named.
- **Appendix A pairings.** The verse set beside each great O antiphon is the book's own pairing; the antiphons are the 1888 Breviary's and the verses the Douay-Rheims.
- **Page-image passages.** Waterworth's Trent, the Pustet de Sales and the USCCB statements are registered as page-image or web passages whose wording stands in the record's context and notes, not in a checked transcription artifact.
- **Scripture.** English verses are the Douay-Rheims (Challoner) in the tracked Project Gutenberg 1581 verse text, Latin the Clementine Vulgate in the tracked eBible latVUC verse text, both read in the tracked file (inspected). Verse loci use the Vulgate numbering.

## Claims the primer must not make

| Key | Claim the primer must not make | Reason | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `x-1962-reads-leo` | The 1962 Office reads Leo's December sermons or Jerome on Isaiah 11 on the Advent Sundays. | One-nocturn Sunday Matins reads two Isaiah lessons and the opening of the homily only. | AAS 52 nn. 162 a, 220 |
| `x-gregory-preached-on-sunday` | Gregory preached Homily 1 (6, 7) on the 1962 Sunday that reads it. | His headings place them a week later in his calendar; Homily 20 was an Ember Saturday sermon. | Wikisource 7.2 record (inspected) |
| `x-leo-advent` | Leo preached on "Advent". | He preached on the fast of the tenth month; "Advent" is Bute's and NPNF's word. | BR88 leo-ember-notice; PL 54 |
| `x-saragossa-fast` | Saragossa (380) or Tours (567) imposed an Advent fast on the faithful. | Saragossa enjoins attendance; Tours is monastic. | Hefele II pp. 292-293; Maassen p. 126 |
| `x-macon-one-date` | Mâcon c. 9 is of 581 (or 582, 583) without qualification. | The witnesses disagree. | Hefele IV pp. 403-404, Guéranger, Maassen |
| `x-x31-book-ii` | Perpetuus is in Book II of the History of the Franks. | It is X.31; Guéranger's English is wrong. | MGH, Dalton |
| `x-x31-unanimous` | All manuscripts of X.31 carry the Martinmas fast line. | Guadet-Taranne n. 1 records that the article is not in the codex Regius. | Guadet-Taranne |
| `x-gregory-vii` | Gregory VII fixed four weeks. | Amalarius reports belief that Gregory I authored the four-week Missal. | PL 105 col. 1219 |
| `x-four-weeks-fact` | Gregory I certainly established the four weeks. | Amalarius says "credimus". | PL 105 col. 1219 |
| `x-six-sundays-rome` | Rome once kept six Sundays. | Six are Ambrosian; the Gelasian has five Masses; the Hadrianum four Sundays. | Wilson 1894, 1915; Magistretti |
| `x-three-excita` | There are three Excita collects. | There are four (Advent I, II, IV, Ember Friday). | editorial projection records |
| `x-every-feria-sunday-mass` | Every weekday of Advent takes the Mass of the preceding Sunday. | The Ember ferias have their own Mass, and the rubrics provide otherwise for feasts kept; the Vigil is not a feria. | AAS 52 n. 299 |
| `x-o-virgo-virginum` | O Virgo virginum is a Roman O antiphon. | Only the seven, 17-23 December; O Virgo virginum belongs to a local Expectation office. | BR88 p. 156 |
| `x-rorate-roman-chant` | The Rorate with verses is a Roman Office or Mass text, or its origin is known. | Guéranger prints it as a prayer of the Churches of France; MGC as a Benediction chant; origin not established. | Guéranger p. 155; MGC p. 313 |
| `x-rorate-mass-rubric` | The 1962 rubrics allow a votive Rorate Mass on any Advent weekday; or a Rorate Mass custom, with time and place. | IV-class votive Masses need IV-class days, and no Advent weekday is IV class; n. 384 is unsettled for a III-class feria; no witness documents the custom. | AAS 52 nn. 384, 387; nn. 24-25 |
| `x-advent-fast-obligation` | Advent itself binds to fast under 1917 or 1983 law. | Neither Code names Advent; only the Ember days and Vigil bound to fast under 1917. | CIC 1917 c. 1252; CIC 1983 cc. 1250-1253 |
| `x-friday-holy-day-1917` | A Friday that was a holy day of obligation (8 December) was a day of abstinence under the 1917 Code. | The law of abstinence ceased on holy days of obligation outside Lent. | CIC 1917 c. 1252 §§1, 4 |
| `x-us-ember-vigil-now` | US Catholics today are bound to fast on Advent Ember days or the Christmas Vigil. | The 1966 statement lifted them; it remains in force under the 1983 complementary norm. | USCCB ¶17; complementary norm |
| `x-us-advent-fridays-1917` | The US Advent Friday fast was still in force after 1918. | Unresolved: no witness held for 1918-1966 US practice. | Manual 1889 only |
| `x-english-fast-verified` | The English Wednesday-Friday Advent fast of 1870 is verified law. | Only a translator's note in Guéranger 1870; no law text held. | Guéranger p. 28 n. 1 |
| `x-byzantine-primary` | Details of the Byzantine Nativity Fast as primary-sourced. | Only Guéranger's secondary description is held. | Guéranger p. 27 |
| `x-lings-promise` | The St Andrew prayer carries a promise or indulgence, or comes from Ireland. | Lings prints "It is piously believed"; no Irish witness found. | Lings p. 29 |
| `x-merge-angelus` | A merged Angelus from the Raccolta and the 1889 Manual. | The Manual has no Ora pro nobis; never merge witnesses. | Raccolta 1910; Manual 1889 |
| `x-de-sales-rivingtons` | Any quotation of de Sales from the Rivingtons 1876 translation. | It is Anglican-edited; quote only the Pustet (New York) English. | coordinator correction 2026-11-18 |
| `x-scala-guigo-certain` | Guigo II certainly wrote the Scala claustralium. | PL 184 ascribes it with a query. | PL 184 col. 475 |
| `x-bernard-second-coming` | Bernard's "second advent" in Sermon V means the Second Coming. | In Melleray's English of Sermon V it is the hidden middle coming. | Melleray V.1 |
| `x-catena-pure` | Catena excerpts are continuous text of one homily. | Luke 3:4 joins Gregory 7.2 and 20.3; Luke 1:39 joins Ambrose II.19 to a condensed II.21; Luke 1:46-47 starts at the end of II.27. | Catena records |
| `x-catena-cyril-jerusalem` | The Catena's "Cyril" on St Luke is Cyril of Jerusalem. | It is Cyril of Alexandria (Commentary on Luke, Sermon 1). | Catena on Luke 2:7; Payne Smith 1859 (not held) |
| `x-bede-latin-house-of-bread` | A Latin text for Bede's "house of eternal bread" as verified. | The line is quoted in the Catena's English only; no verified Bede passage holds it. | Catena on Luke 2:6-7 |
| `x-bute-numbering` | Bute's homily numbers. | Bute heads Hom. 6 "10th"; cite the standard numbering. | Bute p. 229 |
| `x-own-translation-unlisted` | An English rendering of a Latin passage not listed in Appendix C as the book's own. | Received prayer and quotation only from identified witnesses; the book's own renderings are listed. | Appendix C; guidance/editorial.md |
| `x-office-clock-hours` | The proper clock hours of the Hours (midnight, dawn, 3 P.M., sunset). | Bute's Psalter footnotes were read on page images only and are not registered. | none held |
| `x-adventus-etymology` | Sourced claims on adventus, parousia or imperial arrival. | No lexicon or classical witness is held; Bernard I.1 may carry the name. | none held |
| `x-wreath-jesse` | Origins of the Advent wreath or Jesse tree. | Unverified leads only. | none held |
| `x-aas52-facsimile` | That every AAS 52 rubric was checked on a facsimile without qualification. | The earlier AAS 52 passage records say "microfilm page image", but the vatican.va rendering is a re-typesetting and the relation of each record to the registered facsimile is unresolved; n. 299 alone was transcribed from the registered facsimile on 2026-11-19. | AAS 52 records |

## Law statements

Each discipline the primer states, with its body of law, territory and date. "As of" means checked against the held text for the primer's as-of date, 29 November 2026.

- **Universal Latin law, 1918-1983 (1917 Code).** Territory: Latin Church, worldwide. Date: in force 19 May 1918 to 26 Nov 1983. can. 1252 §1: abstinence alone on every Friday; §2: fast and abstinence on the Ember days and the Vigil of the Nativity; §4: the law ceases on Sundays and on holy days of obligation outside Lent, and vigils are not anticipated. can. 1108 §2: solemn nuptial blessing forbidden Advent I to Christmas inclusive.
- **S. C. Concilii decree, 3 Dec 1959 (AAS 51, 918).** Territory: Latin Church, worldwide. Date: from 3 Dec 1959. Faculty to anticipate the Vigil fast and abstinence to 23 December.
- **1960 Code of Rubrics (AAS 52).** Territory: Roman Rite. Date: from 1 Jan 1961; the 1962 books. Advent bounds (n. 71), first Sunday (n. 20), ranks (nn. 11, 24-25), precedence (nn. 15, 30), colours (n. 131), Matins (nn. 162 a, 169, 213, 220, 221 a, 222 a), commemorations (n. 109), the ferial Mass (Rubricae generales Missalis n. 299), votive Masses (nn. 384, 387), Ite missa est (n. 507).
- **US particular discipline, 1889 (Baltimore Manual).** Territory: United States; some dioceses excepted (Province of New Orleans). Date: 1889; survival after 1918 unresolved. Fridays of Advent fast days; Ember days; Vigil of Christmas.
- **Paenitemini, 17 Feb 1966.** Territory: Universal Church. Date: from 1966. Penitential days: Fridays and Lent (Ash Wednesday); no Advent day named; conferences may substitute.
- **NCCB pastoral statement, 18 Nov 1966.** Territory: United States. Date: effective 27 Nov 1966; in force as of 2026-11-29. Advent: meditation and liturgical participation (¶7); vigils and Ember days no longer oblige (¶17); Friday remains penitential (¶¶22-25).
- **1983 Code, cc. 1246-1247.** Territory: Latin Church, worldwide. Date: from 27 Nov 1983; as of 2026-11-29. Sunday and the holy days of obligation, the Immaculate Conception among them unless a conference has suppressed it or moved it to a Sunday (c. 1246 §2); Mass on those days (c. 1247).
- **1983 Code, cc. 1250-1253.** Territory: Latin Church, worldwide. Date: from 27 Nov 1983; as of 2026-11-29. Penitential days: Fridays and Lent; no Advent day named.
- **USCCB complementary norm to cc. 1252-1253 (1983; amended 1984).** Territory: United States. Date: from 1983/1984; as of 2026-11-29. Keeps the 1966 norms in force; fasting "from the completion of the eighteenth year to the beginning of the sixtieth" (1984 amendment).
- **S. C. Rites, instruction on sacred music, 1958 (AAS 50, p. 654).** Territory: Roman Rite. Date: 1958; the 1962 books. Instruments silent in Advent except at eucharistic Benediction (n. 81 a); exceptions in n. 83 a-c.
